   81: =head1 NAME
   82: 
   83: App::Test::Generator::SchemaExtractor - Extract test schemas from Perl modules
   84: 
   85: =head1 VERSION
   86: 
   87: Version 0.49
   88: 
   89: =cut
   90: 
   91: our $VERSION = '0.49';
   92: 
   93: =head1 SYNOPSIS
   94: 
   95: 	use App::Test::Generator::SchemaExtractor;
   96: 
   97: 	my $extractor = App::Test::Generator::SchemaExtractor->new(
   98: 		input_file => 'lib/MyModule.pm',
   99: 		output_dir => 'schemas/',
  100: 		verbose	=> 1,
  101: 	);
  102: 
  103: 	my $schemas = $extractor->extract_all();
  104: 
  105: =head1 DESCRIPTION
  106: 
  107: App::Test::Generator::SchemaExtractor analyzes Perl modules and generates
  108: structured YAML schema files suitable for automated test generation by L<App::Test::Generator>.
  109: This module employs
  110: static analysis techniques to infer parameter types, constraints, and
  111: method behaviors directly from your source code.
  112: 
  113: =head2 Analysis Methods
  114: 
  115: The extractor combines multiple analysis approaches for a comprehensive schema generation:
  116: 
  117: =over 4
  118: 
  119: =item * B<POD Documentation Analysis>
  120: 
  121: Parses embedded documentation to extract:
  122:   - Parameter names, types, and descriptions from =head2 sections
  123:   - Method signatures with positional parameters
  124:   - Return value specifications from "Returns:" sections
  125:   - Constraints (ranges, patterns, required/optional status)
  126:   - Semantic type detection (email, URL, filename)
  127: 
  128: =item * B<Code Pattern Detection>
  129: 
  130: Analyzes source code using PPI to identify:
  131:   - Method signatures and parameter extraction patterns
  132:   - Type validation (ref(), isa(), blessed())
  133:   - Constraint patterns (length checks, numeric comparisons, regex matches)
  134:   - Return statement analysis and value type inference
  135:   - Object instantiation requirements and accessor methods
  136: 
  137: =item * B<Signature Analysis>
  138: 
  139: Examines method declarations for:
  140:   - Parameter names and positional information
  141:   - Instance vs. class method detection
  142:   - Method modifiers (Moose-style before/after/around)
  143:   - Various parameter declaration styles (shift, @_ assignment)
  144: 
  145: =item * B<Heuristic Inference>
  146: 
  147: Applies Perl-specific domain knowledge:
  148:   - Boolean return detection from method names (is_*, has_*, can_*)
  149:   - Common Perl idioms and coding patterns
  150:   - Context awareness (scalar vs list, wantarray usage)
  151:   - Object-oriented patterns (constructors, accessors, chaining)
  152: 
  153: =back
  154: 
  155: =head2 Generated Schema Structure
  156: 
  157: The extracted schemas follow this YAML structure:
  158: 
  159:     function: method_name
  160:     module: Package::Name
  161:     input:
  162:       param1:
  163:         type: string
  164:         min: 3
  165:         max: 50
  166:         optional: 0
  167:         position: 0
  168:       param2:
  169:         type: integer
  170:         min: 0
  171:         max: 100
  172:         optional: 1
  173:         position: 1
  174:     output:
  175:       type: boolean
  176:       value: 1
  177:     new: Package::Name # if object instantiation required
  178:     config:
  179:       test_empty: 1
  180:       test_nuls: 0
  181:       test_undef: 0
  182:       test_non_ascii: 0
  183: 
  184: =head2 Advanced Detection Capabilities
  185: 
  186: =over 4
  187: 
  188: =item * B<Accessor Method Detection>
  189: 
  190: Automatically identifies getter, setter, and combined accessor methods
  191: by analyzing common patterns like C<return $self-E<gt>{property}> and
  192: C<$self-E<gt>{property} = $value>.
  193: 
  194: =item * B<Params::Get Integration>
  195: 
  196: Recognises parameters extracted via C<Params::Get::get_params('key', \@_)>,
  197: treating the quoted key as a named parameter equivalent to a traditional
  198: C<my ($self, $key) = @_> signature.  This prevents false positives from
  199: C<--strict-pod> when the method body never declares an explicit C<$key>
  200: variable.
  201: 
  202: =item * B<Direct-Index Self Style>
  203: 
  204: Recognises C<my $self = $_[0]> as a valid method-invocant pattern.  Parameters
  205: at C<$_[1]>, C<$_[2]>, etc. are extracted as positional parameters.  Without
  206: this, the signature fallback would incorrectly pick up C<my (...) = @_> from
  207: inner closures defined in the method body and treat those variables as the
  208: outer method's parameters.
  209: 
  210: =item * B<Boolean Return Inference>
  211: 
  212: Detects boolean-returning methods through multiple signals:
  213:   - Method name patterns (is_*, has_*, can_*)
  214:   - Return patterns (consistent 1/0 returns)
  215:   - POD descriptions ("returns true on success")
  216:   - Ternary operators with boolean results
  217: 
  218: =item * B<Context Awareness>
  219: 
  220: Identifies methods that use C<wantarray> and can return different
  221: values in scalar vs list context.
  222: 
  223: =item * B<Object Lifecycle Management>
  224: 
  225: Detects instance methods requiring object instantiation and
  226: automatically adds the C<new> field to schemas.
  227: 
  228: =item * B<Enhanced Object Detection>
  229: 
  230: The extractor includes sophisticated object detection capabilities that go beyond simple instance method identification:
  231: 
  232: =over 4
  233: 
  234: =item * B<Factory Method Recognition>
  235: 
  236: Automatically identifies methods that create and return object instances, such as methods named C<create_*>, C<make_*>, C<build_*>, or C<get_*>. Factory methods are correctly classified as class methods that don't require pre-existing objects for testing.
  237: 
  238: =item * B<Singleton Pattern Detection>
  239: 
  240: Recognizes singleton patterns through multiple signals: method names like C<instance> or C<get_instance>, static variables holding instance references, lazy initialization patterns (C<$instance ||= new()>), and consistent return of the same instance variable.
  241: 
  242: =item * B<Constructor Parameter Analysis>
  243: 
  244: Examines C<new> methods to determine required and optional parameters, validation requirements, and default values. This enables test generators to provide appropriate constructor arguments when object instantiation is needed.
  245: 
  246: =item * B<Inheritance Relationship Handling>
  247: 
  248: Detects parent classes through C<use parent>, C<use base>, and C<@ISA> declarations. Identifies when methods use C<SUPER::> calls and determines whether the current class or a parent class constructor should be used for object instantiation.
  249: 
  250: =item * B<External Object Dependency Detection>
  251: 
  252: Identifies when methods create or depend on objects from other classes, enabling proper test setup with mock objects or real dependencies.
  253: 
  254: =back
  255: 
  256: These enhancements ensure that generated test schemas accurately reflect the object-oriented structure of the code, leading to more meaningful and effective test generation.
  257: 
  258: =back
  259: 
  260: =head2 Confidence Scoring
  261: 
  262: Each generated schema includes detailed confidence assessments:
  263: 
  264: =over 4
  265: 
  266: =item * B<High Confidence>
  267: 
  268: Multiple independent analysis sources converge on consistent,
  269: well-constrained parameters with explicit validation logic and
  270: comprehensive documentation.
  271: 
  272: =item * B<Medium Confidence>
  273: 
  274: Reasonable evidence from code patterns or partial documentation,
  275: but may lack comprehensive constraints or have some ambiguities.
  276: 
  277: =item * B<Low Confidence>
  278: 
  279: Minimal evidence - primarily based on naming conventions,
  280: default assumptions, or single-source analysis.
  281: 
  282: =item * B<Very Low Confidence>
  283: 
  284: Barely any detectable signals - schema should be thoroughly
  285: reviewed before use in test generation.
  286: 
  287: =back
  288: 
  289: =head2 Use Cases
  290: 
  291: =over 4
  292: 
  293: =item * B<Automated Test Generation>
  294: 
  295: Generate comprehensive test suites with L<App::Test::Generator> using
  296: extracted schemas as input. The schemas provide the necessary structure
  297: for generating both positive and negative test cases.
  298: 
  299: =item * B<API Documentation Generation>
  300: 
  301: Supplement existing documentation with automatically inferred interface
  302: specifications, parameter requirements, and return types.
  303: 
  304: =item * B<Code Quality Assessment>
  305: 
  306: Identify methods with poor documentation, inconsistent parameter handling,
  307: or unclear interfaces that may benefit from refactoring.
  308: 
  309: =item * B<Refactoring Assistance>
  310: 
  311: Detect method dependencies, object instantiation requirements, and
  312: parameter usage patterns to inform refactoring decisions.
  313: 
  314: =item * B<Legacy Code Analysis>
  315: 
  316: Quickly understand the interface contracts of legacy Perl codebases
  317: without extensive manual code reading.
  318: 
  319: =back
  320: 
  321: =head2 Integration with Testing Ecosystem
  322: 
  323: The generated schemas are specifically designed to work with the
  324: L<App::Test::Generator> ecosystem:
  325: 
  326:     # Extract schemas from your module
  327:     my $extractor = App::Test::Generator::SchemaExtractor->new(...);
  328:     my $schemas = $extractor->extract_all();
  329: 
  330:     # Use with test generator (typically as separate steps)
  331:     # fuzz-harness-generator -r schemas/method_name.yml
  332: 
  333: =head2 Limitations and Considerations
  334: 
  335: =over 4
  336: 
  337: =item * B<Dynamic Code Patterns>
  338: 
  339: Highly dynamic code (string evals, AUTOLOAD, symbolic references)
  340: may not be fully detected by static analysis.
  341: 
  342: =item * B<Complex Validation Logic>
  343: 
  344: Sophisticated validation involving multiple parameters or external
  345: dependencies may require manual schema refinement.
  346: 
  347: =item * B<Confidence Heuristics>
  348: 
  349: Confidence scores are based on heuristics and should be reviewed
  350: by developers familiar with the codebase.
  351: 
  352: =item * B<Perl Idiom Recognition>
  353: 
  354: Some Perl-specific idioms may require custom pattern recognition
  355: beyond the built-in detectors.
  356: 
  357: =item * B<Documentation Dependency>
  358: 
  359: Analysis quality improves significantly with comprehensive POD
  360: documentation following consistent patterns.
  361: 
  362: =back
  363: 
  364: =head2 Best Practices for Optimal Results
  365: 
  366: =over 4
  367: 
  368: =item * B<Comprehensive POD Documentation>
  369: 
  370: Write detailed POD with explicit parameter documentation using
  371: consistent patterns like C<$param - type (constraints), description>.
  372: 
  373: =item * B<Consistent Coding Patterns>
  374: 
  375: Use consistent parameter validation patterns and method signatures
  376: throughout your codebase.
  377: 
  378: =item * B<Schema Review Process>
  379: 
  380: Review and refine automatically generated schemas, particularly
  381: those with low confidence scores.
  382: 
  383: =item * B<Descriptive Naming>
  384: 
  385: Use descriptive method and parameter names that clearly indicate
  386: purpose and expected types.
  387: 
  388: =item * B<Progressive Enhancement>
  389: 
  390: Start with automatically generated schemas and progressively
  391: refine them based on test results and code understanding.
  392: 
  393: =back
  394: 
  395: The module is particularly valuable for large codebases where manual schema
  396: creation would be prohibitively time-consuming, and for maintaining test
  397: coverage as code evolves through continuous integration pipelines.
  398: 
  399: =head2 Advanced Type Detection
  400: 
  401: The schema extractor includes enhanced type detection capabilities that identify specialized Perl types beyond basic strings and integers.
  402: L<DateTime> and L<Time::Piece> objects are detected through isa() checks and method call patterns, while date strings (ISO 8601, YYYY-MM-DD) and UNIX timestamps are recognized through regex validation and numeric range checks.
  403: File handles and file paths are identified via I/O operations and file test operators, coderefs are detected through ref() checks and invocation patterns, and enum-like parameters are extracted from validation code including regex patterns (C</^(a|b|c)$/>), hash lookups, grep statements, and if/elsif chains.
  404: These detected types are preserved in the generated YAML schemas with appropriate semantic annotations, enabling test generators to create more accurate and meaningful test cases.
  405: 
  406: =head3 Example Advanced Type Schema
  407: 
  408: For a method like:
  409: 
  410:     sub process_event {
  411:         my ($self, $timestamp, $status, $callback) = @_;
  412:         croak unless $timestamp > 1000000000;
  413:         croak unless $status =~ /^(active|pending|complete)$/;
  414:         croak unless ref($callback) eq 'CODE';
  415:         $callback->($timestamp, $status);
  416:     }
  417: 
  418: The extractor generates:
  419: 
  420:     ---
  421:     function: process_event
  422:     module: MyModule
  423:     input:
  424:       timestamp:
  425:         type: integer
  426:         # min: 0
  427:         # max: 2147483647
  428:         position: 0
  429:         _note: Unix timestamp
  430: 	semantic: unix_timestamp
  431:       status:
  432:         type: string
  433:         enum:
  434:           - active
  435:           - pending
  436:           - complete
  437:         position: 1
  438:         _note: 'Must be one of: active, pending, complete'
  439:       callback:
  440:         type: coderef
  441:         position: 2
  442:         _note: 'CODE reference - provide sub { } in tests'
  443: 
  444: =head1 RELATIONSHIP DETECTION
  445: 
  446: The schema extractor detects relationships and dependencies between parameters,
  447: enabling more sophisticated validation and test generation.
  448: 
  449: =head2 Relationship Types
  450: 
  451: =over 4
  452: 
  453: =item * B<mutually_exclusive>
  454: 
  455: Parameters that cannot be used together.
  456: 
  457:     die if $file && $content;  # Can't specify both
  458: 
  459: Generated schema:
  460: 
  461:     relationships:
  462:       - type: mutually_exclusive
  463:         params: [file, content]
  464:         description: Cannot specify both file and content
  465: 
  466: =item * B<required_group>
  467: 
  468: At least one parameter from the group must be specified (OR logic).
  469: 
  470:     die unless $id || $name;  # Must provide one
  471: 
  472: Generated schema:
  473: 
  474:     relationships:
  475:       - type: required_group
  476:         params: [id, name]
  477:         logic: or
  478:         description: Must specify either id or name
  479: 
  480: =item * B<conditional_requirement>
  481: 
  482: If one parameter is specified, another becomes required (IF-THEN logic).
  483: 
  484:     die if $async && !$callback;  # async requires callback
  485: 
  486: Generated schema:
  487: 
  488:     relationships:
  489:       - type: conditional_requirement
  490:         if: async
  491:         then_required: callback
  492:         description: When async is specified, callback is required
  493: 
  494: =item * B<dependency>
  495: 
  496: One parameter depends on another being present.
  497: 
  498:     die "Port requires host" if $port && !$host;
  499: 
  500: Generated schema:
  501: 
  502:     relationships:
  503:       - type: dependency
  504:         param: port
  505:         requires: host
  506:         description: port requires host to be specified
  507: 
  508: =item * B<value_constraint>
  509: 
  510: Specific value requirements between parameters.
  511: 
  512:     die if $ssl && $port != 443;  # ssl requires port 443
  513: 
  514: Generated schema:
  515: 
  516:     relationships:
  517:       - type: value_constraint
  518:         if: ssl
  519:         then: port
  520:         operator: ==
  521:         value: 443
  522:         description: When ssl is specified, port must equal 443
  523: 
  524: =item * B<value_conditional>
  525: 
  526: Parameter required when another has a specific value.
  527: 
  528:     die if $mode eq 'secure' && !$key;
  529: 
  530: Generated schema:
  531: 
  532:     relationships:
  533:       - type: value_conditional
  534:         if: mode
  535:         equals: secure
  536:         then_required: key
  537:         description: When mode equals 'secure', key is required
  538: 
  539: =back
  540: 
  541: =head2 Default Value Extraction
  542: 
  543: The extractor comprehensively extracts default values from both code and POD documentation:
  544: 
  545: =head3 Code Pattern Recognition
  546: 
  547: Extracts defaults from multiple Perl idioms:
  548: 
  549: =over 4
  550: 
  551: =item * Logical OR operator: C<$param = $param || 'default'>
  552: 
  553: =item * Defined-or operator: C<$param //= 'default'>
  554: 
  555: =item * Ternary operator: C<$param = defined $param ? $param : 'default'>
  556: 
  557: =item * Unless conditional: C<$param = 'default' unless defined $param>
  558: 
  559: =item * Chained defaults: C<$param = $param || $self->{_default} || 'fallback'>
  560: 
  561: =item * Multi-line patterns: C<$param = {} unless $param>
  562: 
  563: =back
  564: 
  565: =head3 POD Pattern Recognition
  566: 
  567: Extracts defaults from documentation:
  568: 
  569: =over 4
  570: 
  571: =item * Standard format: C<Default: 'value'>
  572: 
  573: =item * Alternative format: C<Defaults to: 'value'>
  574: 
  575: =item * Inline format: C<Optional, default: 'value'>
  576: 
  577: =item * Parameter lists: C<$param - type, default 'value'>
  578: 
  579: =back
  580: 
  581: =head3 Value Processing
  582: 
  583: Properly handles:
  584: 
  585: =over 4
  586: 
  587: =item * String literals with quotes and escape sequences
  588: 
  589: =item * Numeric values (integers and floats)
  590: 
  591: =item * Boolean values (true/false converted to 1/0)
  592: 
  593: =item * Empty data structures ([] and {})
  594: 
  595: =item * Special values (undef, __PACKAGE__)
  596: 
  597: =item * Complex expressions (preserved as-is when unevaluatable)
  598: 
  599: =item * Quote operators (q{}, qq{}, qw{})
  600: 
  601: =back
  602: 
  603: =head3 Type Inference
  604: 
  605: When a parameter has a default value but no explicit type annotation,
  606: the type is automatically inferred from the default:
  607: 
  608:     $options = {}        # inferred as hashref
  609:     $items = []          # inferred as arrayref
  610:     $count = 42          # inferred as integer
  611:     $ratio = 3.14        # inferred as number
  612:     $enabled = 1         # inferred as boolean
  613: 
  614: =head2 Context-Aware Return Analysis
  615: 
  616: The extractor provides comprehensive analysis of method return behavior,
  617: including context sensitivity, error handling conventions, and method chaining patterns.
  618: 
  619: When a method's POD contains a C<=head4 Output> block in
  620: L<Params::Validate::Strict> schema format, the C<type> declared there is
  621: used as the authoritative output type and takes precedence over all
  622: heuristic code analysis:
  623: 
  624:     =head4 Output
  625: 
  626:         {
  627:             type => 'hashref',
  628:         }
  629: 
  630: This is the recommended way to document methods whose return type would
  631: otherwise be misidentified (e.g. a method that returns C<$self-E<gt>{cache}>
  632: where the cache happens to hold a hashref).
  633: 
  634: Using parentheses as the outer container emits C<type: array>, indicating a
  635: list-returning method.  L<App::Test::Generator> 0.39+ (with L<Test::Returns>
  636: 0.03+) captures these results in list context automatically:
  637: 
  638:     =head4 Output
  639: 
  640:         (
  641:             {
  642:                 type => 'hashref',
  643:             },
  644:             ...
  645:         )
  646: 
  647: =head3 List vs Scalar Context Detection
  648: 
  649: Automatically detects methods that return different values based on calling context:
  650: 
  651:     sub get_items {
  652:         my $self = $_[0];
  653:         return wantarray ? @items : scalar(@items);
  654:     }
  655: 
  656: Detection captures:
  657: 
  658: =over 4
  659: 
  660: =item * C<_context_aware> flag - Method uses wantarray
  661: 
  662: =item * C<_list_context> - Type returned in list context (e.g., 'array')
  663: 
  664: =item * C<_scalar_context> - Type returned in scalar context (e.g., 'integer')
  665: 
  666: =back
  667: 
  668: Recognizes both ternary operator patterns and conditional return patterns.
  669: 
  670: =head3 Void Context Methods
  671: 
  672: Identifies methods that don't return meaningful values:
  673: 
  674: =over 4
  675: 
  676: =item * Setters (C<set_*> methods)
  677: 
  678: =item * Mutators (C<add_*, remove_*, delete_*, clear_*, reset_*, update_*>)
  679: 
  680: =item * Loggers (C<log, debug, warn, error, info>)
  681: 
  682: =item * Methods with only empty returns
  683: 
  684: =back
  685: 
  686: Example:
  687: 
  688:     sub set_name {
  689:         my ($self, $name) = @_;
  690:         $self->{name} = $name;
  691:         return;  # Void context
  692:     }
  693: 
  694: Sets C<_void_context> flag and C<type =E<gt> 'void'>.
  695: 
  696: =head3 Method Chaining Detection
  697: 
  698: Identifies chainable methods that return C<$self> for fluent interfaces:
  699: 
  700:     sub set_width {
  701:         my ($self, $width) = @_;
  702:         $self->{width} = $width;
  703:         return $self;  # Chainable
  704:     }
  705: 
  706: Detection provides:
  707: 
  708: =over 4
  709: 
  710: =item * C<_returns_self> - Returns invocant for chaining
  711: 
  712: =item * C<class> - The class name being returned
  713: 
  714: =back
  715: 
  716: Also detects chaining documentation in POD (keywords: "chainable", "fluent interface",
  717: "returns self", "method chaining").
  718: 
  719: =head3 Error Return Conventions
  720: 
  721: Analyzes how methods signal errors:
  722: 
  723: B<Pattern Detection:>
  724: 
  725: =over 4
  726: 
  727: =item * C<undef_on_error> - Explicit C<return undef if/unless condition>
  728: 
  729: =item * C<implicit_undef> - Bare C<return if/unless condition>
  730: 
  731: =item * C<empty_list> - C<return ()> for list context errors
  732: 
  733: =item * C<zero_on_error> - Returns 0/false for boolean error indication
  734: 
  735: =item * C<exception_handling> - Uses eval blocks with error checking
  736: 
  737: =back
  738: 
  739: B<Example Analysis:>
  740: 
  741:     sub fetch_user {
  742:         my ($self, $id) = @_;
  743: 
  744:         return undef unless $id;        # undef_on_error
  745:         return undef if $id < 0;        # undef_on_error
  746: 
  747:         return $self->{users}{$id};
  748:     }
  749: 
  750: Results in:
  751: 
  752:     _error_return: 'undef'
  753:     _success_failure_pattern: 1
  754:     _error_handling: {
  755:         undef_on_error: ['$id', '$id < 0']
  756:     }
  757: 
  758: B<Success/Failure Pattern:>
  759: 
  760: Methods that return different types for success vs. failure are flagged with
  761: C<_success_failure_pattern>. Common patterns:
  762: 
  763: =over 4
  764: 
  765: =item * Returns value on success, undef on failure
  766: 
  767: =item * Returns true on success, false on failure
  768: 
  769: =item * Returns data on success, empty list on failure
  770: 
  771: =back
  772: 
  773: =head3 Success Indicator Detection
  774: 
  775: Methods that always return true (typically for side effects):
  776: 
  777:     sub update_status {
  778:         my ($self, $status) = @_;
  779:         $self->{status} = $status;
  780:         return 1;  # Success indicator
  781:     }
  782: 
  783: Sets C<_success_indicator> flag when method consistently returns 1.
  784: 
  785: =head3 Schema Output
  786: 
  787: Enhanced return analysis adds these fields to method schemas:
  788: 
  789:     output:
  790:       type: boolean              # Inferred return type
  791:       _context_aware: 1           # Uses wantarray
  792:       _list_context:
  793:         type: array
  794:       _scalar_context:
  795:         type: integer
  796:       _returns_self: 1               # Returns $self
  797:       _void_context: 1            # No meaningful return
  798:       _success_indicator: 1       # Always returns true
  799:       _error_return: undef        # How errors are signaled
  800:       _success_failure_pattern: 1 # Mixed return types
  801:       _error_handling:            # Detailed error patterns
  802:         undef_on_error: [...]
  803:         exception_handling: 1
  804: 
  805: This comprehensive analysis enables:
  806: 
  807: =over 4
  808: 
  809: =item * Better test generation (testing both contexts, error paths)
  810: 
  811: =item * Documentation generation (clear error conventions)
  812: 
  813: =item * API design validation (consistent error handling)
  814: 
  815: =item * Contract specification (precise return behavior)
  816: 
  817: =back
  818: 
  819: =head2 Example
  820: 
  821: For a method like:
  822: 
  823:     sub connect {
  824:         my ($self, $host, $port, $ssl, $file, $content) = @_;
  825: 
  826:         die if $file && $content;                    # mutually exclusive
  827:         die unless $host || $file;                   # required group
  828:         die "Port requires host" if $port && !$host; # dependency
  829:         die if $ssl && $port != 443;                 # value constraint
  830: 
  831:         # ... connection logic
  832:     }
  833: 
  834: The extractor generates:
  835: 
  836:     relationships:
  837:       - type: mutually_exclusive
  838:         params: [file, content]
  839:         description: Cannot specify both file and content
  840:       - type: required_group
  841:         params: [host, file]
  842:         logic: or
  843:         description: Must specify either host or file
  844:       - type: dependency
  845:         param: port
  846:         requires: host
  847:         description: port requires host to be specified
  848:       - type: value_constraint
  849:         if: ssl
  850:         then: port
  851:         operator: ==
  852:         value: 443
  853:         description: When ssl is specified, port must equal 443
  854: 
  855: =head1 MODERN PERL FEATURES
  856: 
  857: This module adds support for:
  858: 
  859: =head2 Subroutine Signatures (Perl 5.20+)
  860: 
  861:     sub connect($host, $port = 3306, %options) {
  862:         ...
  863:     }
  864: 
  865: Extracts: required params, optional params with defaults, slurpy params
  866: 
  867: =head2 Type Constraints (Perl 5.36+)
  868: 
  869:     sub calculate($x :Int, $y :Num) {
  870:         ...
  871:     }
  872: 
  873: Recognizes: Int, Num, Str, Bool, ArrayRef, HashRef, custom classes
  874: 
  875: =head3 Subroutine Attributes
  876: 
  877:     sub get_value :lvalue :Returns(Int) {
  878:         ...
  879:     }
  880: 
  881: Detects: :lvalue, :method, :Returns(Type), custom attributes
  882: 
  883: =head2 Postfix Dereferencing (Perl 5.20+)
  884: 
  885:     my @array = $arrayref->@*;
  886:     my %hash = $hashref->%*;
  887:     my @slice = $arrayref->@[1,3,5];
  888: 
  889: Tracks usage of modern dereferencing syntax
  890: 
  891: =head2 Field Declarations (Perl 5.38+)
  892: 
  893:     field $host :param = 'localhost';
  894:     field $port :param(port_number) = 3306;
  895:     field $logger :param :isa(Log::Any);
  896: 
  897: Extracts fields and maps them to parameters
  898: 
  899: =head2 Modern Perl Features Support
  900: 
  901: The schema extractor supports modern Perl syntax introduced in versions 5.20, 5.36, and 5.38+.
  902: 
  903: =head3 Subroutine Signatures (Perl 5.20+)
  904: 
  905: Automatically extracts parameters from native Perl signatures:
  906: 
  907:     use feature 'signatures';
  908: 
  909:     sub connect($host, $port = 3306, $database = undef) {
  910:         ...
  911:     }
  912: 
  913: Extracted schema includes:
  914: 
  915: =over 4
  916: 
  917: =item * Parameter positions
  918: 
  919: =item * Optional vs required parameters
  920: 
  921: =item * Default values from signature
  922: 
  923: =item * Slurpy parameters (@array, %hash)
  924: 
  925: =back
  926: 
  927: B<Example:>
  928: 
  929:     # Signature with defaults
  930:     sub process($file, %options) { ... }
  931: 
  932:     # Extracts:
  933:     # $file: position 0, required
  934:     # %options: position 1, optional, slurpy hash
  935: 
  936: =head3 Type Constraints in Signatures (Perl 5.36+)
  937: 
  938: Recognizes type constraints in signature parameters:
  939: 
  940:     sub calculate($x :Int, $y :Num, $name :Str = "result") {
  941:         return $x + $y;
  942:     }
  943: 
  944: Supported constraint types:
  945: 
  946: =over 4
  947: 
  948: =item * C<:Int, :Integer> -> integer
  949: 
  950: =item * C<:Num, :Number> -> number
  951: 
  952: =item * C<:Str, :String> -> string
  953: 
  954: =item * C<:Bool, :Boolean> -> boolean
  955: 
  956: =item * C<:ArrayRef, :Array> -> arrayref
  957: 
  958: =item * C<:HashRef, :Hash> -> hashref
  959: 
  960: =item * C<:ClassName> -> object with isa constraint
  961: 
  962: =back
  963: 
  964: Type constraints are combined with defaults when both are present.
  965: 
  966: =head3 Subroutine Attributes
  967: 
  968: Extracts and documents subroutine attributes:
  969: 
  970:     sub get_value :lvalue {
  971:         my $self = shift;
  972:         return $self->{value};
  973:     }
  974: 
  975:     sub calculate :Returns(Int) :method {
  976:         my ($self, $x, $y) = @_;
  977:         return $x + $y;
  978:     }
  979: 
  980: Recognized attributes stored in C<_attributes> field:
  981: 
  982: =over 4
  983: 
  984: =item * C<:lvalue> - Method can be assigned to
  985: 
  986: =item * C<:method> - Explicitly marked as method
  987: 
  988: =item * C<:Returns(Type)> - Declares return type
  989: 
  990: =item * Custom attributes with values: C<:MyAttr(value)>
  991: 
  992: =back
  993: 
  994: =head3 Postfix Dereferencing (Perl 5.20+)
  995: 
  996: Detects usage of postfix dereferencing syntax:
  997: 
  998:     use feature 'postderef';
  999: 
 1000:     sub process_array {
 1001:         my ($self, $arrayref) = @_;
 1002:         my @array = $arrayref->@*;        # Array dereference
 1003:         my @slice = $arrayref->@[1,3,5];  # Array slice
 1004:         return @array;
 1005:     }
 1006: 
 1007:     sub process_hash {
 1008:         my ($self, $hashref) = @_;
 1009:         my %hash = $hashref->%*;          # Hash dereference
 1010:         return keys %hash;
 1011:     }
 1012: 
 1013: Tracked features stored in C<_modern_features>:
 1014: 
 1015: =over 4
 1016: 
 1017: =item * C<array_deref> - Uses C<-E<gt>@*>
 1018: 
 1019: =item * C<hash_deref> - Uses C<-E<gt>%*>
 1020: 
 1021: =item * C<scalar_deref> - Uses C<-E<gt>$*>
 1022: 
 1023: =item * C<code_deref> - Uses C<-E<gt>&*>
 1024: 
 1025: =item * C<array_slice> - Uses C<-E<gt>@[...]>
 1026: 
 1027: =item * C<hash_slice> - Uses C<-E<gt>%{...}>
 1028: 
 1029: =back
 1030: 
 1031: =head3 Field Declarations (Perl 5.38+)
 1032: 
 1033: Extracts field declarations from class syntax and maps them to method parameters:
 1034: 
 1035:     use feature 'class';
 1036: 
 1037:     class DatabaseConnection {
 1038:         field $host :param = 'localhost';
 1039:         field $port :param = 3306;
 1040:         field $username :param(user);
 1041:         field $password :param;
 1042:         field $logger :param :isa(Log::Any);
 1043: 
 1044:         method connect() {
 1045:             # Fields available as instance variables
 1046:         }
 1047:     }
 1048: 
 1049: Field attributes:
 1050: 
 1051: =over 4
 1052: 
 1053: =item * C<:param> - Field is a constructor parameter (uses field name)
 1054: 
 1055: =item * C<:param(name)> - Field maps to parameter with different name
 1056: 
 1057: =item * C<:isa(Class)> - Type constraint for the field
 1058: 
 1059: =item * Default values in field declarations
 1060: 
 1061: =back
 1062: 
 1063: Extracted schema includes both field information in C<_fields> and merged parameter
 1064: information in C<input>, allowing proper validation of class constructors.
 1065: 
 1066: =head3 Mixed Modern and Traditional Syntax
 1067: 
 1068: The extractor handles code that mixes modern and traditional syntax:
 1069: 
 1070:     sub modern($x, $y = 5) {
 1071:         # Modern signature with default
 1072:     }
 1073: 
 1074:     sub traditional {
 1075:         my ($self, $x, $y) = @_;
 1076:         $y //= 5;  # Traditional default in code
 1077:         # Both extract same parameter information
 1078:     }
 1079: 
 1080: Priority order for parameter information:
 1081: 
 1082: =over 4
 1083: 
 1084: =item 1. Signature declarations (highest priority)
 1085: 
 1086: =item 2. Field declarations (for class methods)
 1087: 
 1088: =item 3. POD documentation
 1089: 
 1090: =item 4. Code analysis (lowest priority)
 1091: 
 1092: =back
 1093: 
 1094: This ensures that explicit declarations in signatures take precedence over
 1095: inferred information from code analysis.
 1096: 
 1097: =head3 Backwards Compatibility
 1098: 
 1099: All modern Perl feature detection is optional and automatic:
 1100: 
 1101: =over 4
 1102: 
 1103: =item * Traditional C<sub> declarations continue to work
 1104: 
 1105: =item * Code without modern features extracts parameters as before
 1106: 
 1107: =item * Modern features are additive - they enhance rather than replace existing extraction
 1108: 
 1109: =item * Each parameter carries one C<_source> value -- C<validator>, C<input_spec>, C<pod>, C<code>, C<signature> or C<field> -- which the harness uses for its type-checking decisions
 1110: 
 1111: =item * Each constraint that can come from more than one place also carries its own provenance in C<_constraint_source>, a hashref keyed by C<min>, C<max>, C<memberof>, C<matches>, C<nomatch> and C<notmemberof>.  A minimum documented in POD and a maximum enforced by the code on the same field therefore stay separate decisions: the documented one is a hint for valid data, the enforced one is a check
 1112: 
 1113: =back
 1114: 
 1115: =head2 _yamltest_hints
 1116: 
 1117: Each method schema returned by L</extract_all> now optionally includes a
 1118: C<_yamltest_hints> key, which provides guidance for automated test generation
 1119: based on the code analysis.
 1120: 
 1121: This is intended to help L<App::Test::Generator> create meaningful tests,
 1122: including boundary and invalid input cases, without manually specifying them.
 1123: 
 1124: The structure is a hashref with the following keys:
 1125: 
 1126: =over 4
 1127: 
 1128: =item * boundary_values
 1129: 
 1130: An arrayref of numeric values that represent boundaries detected from
 1131: comparisons in the code. These are derived from literals in statements
 1132: like C<$x < 0> or C<$y >= 255>. The generator can use these to create
 1133: boundary tests.
 1134: 
 1135: Example:
 1136: 
 1137:     _yamltest_hints:
 1138:       boundary_values: [0, 1, 100, 255]
 1139: 
 1140: =item * invalid_inputs
 1141: 
 1142: An arrayref of values that are likely to be rejected by the method,
 1143: based on checks like C<defined>, empty strings, or numeric validations.
 1144: 
 1145: Example:
 1146: 
 1147:     _yamltest_hints:
 1148:       invalid_inputs: [undef, '', -1]
 1149: 
 1150: =item * equivalence_classes
 1151: 
 1152: An arrayref intended to capture detected equivalence classes or patterns
 1153: among inputs. Currently this is empty by default, but future enhancements
 1154: may populate it based on detected input groupings.
 1155: 
 1156: Example:
 1157: 
 1158:     _yamltest_hints:
 1159:       equivalence_classes: []
 1160: 
 1161: =back
 1162: 
 1163: =head3 Usage
 1164: 
 1165: When calling C<extract_all>, each method schema will include
 1166: C<_yamltest_hints> if any hints were detected:
 1167: 
 1168:     my $schemas = $extractor->extract_all;
 1169:     my $hints  = $schemas->{example_method}->{_yamltest_hints};
 1170: 
 1171: You can then feed these hints into automated test generators to produce
 1172: negative tests, boundary tests, and parameter-specific test cases.
 1173: 
 1174: =head3 Notes
 1175: 
 1176: =over 4
 1177: 
 1178: =item * Hints are inferred heuristically from code and validation statements.
 1179: 
 1180: =item * Not all inputs are guaranteed to be detected; the feature is additive
 1181: and will never remove information from the schema.
 1182: 
 1183: =item * Currently, equivalence classes are not populated, but the field exists
 1184: for future extension.
 1185: 
 1186: =item * Boundary and invalid input hints are deduplicated to avoid repeated
 1187: test values.
 1188: 
 1189: =back
 1190: 
 1191: =head3 Examples
 1192: 
 1193: Given a method like:
 1194: 
 1195:     sub example {
 1196:         my ($x) = @_;
 1197:         die "negative" if $x < 0;
 1198:         return unless defined($x);
 1199:         return $x * 2;
 1200:     }
 1201: 
 1202: After running:
 1203: 
 1204:     my $extractor = App::Test::Generator::SchemaExtractor->new(
 1205:         input_file => 'TestHints.pm',
 1206:         output_dir => '/tmp',
 1207:         quiet    => 1,
 1208:     );
 1209: 
 1210:     my $schemas = $extractor->extract_all;
 1211: 
 1212: The schema for the method "example" will include:
 1213: 
 1214:     $schemas->{example} = {
 1215:         function => 'example',
 1216:         _confidence => {
 1217:             input  => 'unknown',
 1218:             output => 'unknown',
 1219:         },
 1220:         input => {
 1221:             x => {
 1222:                 type     => 'scalar',
 1223:                 optional => 0,
 1224:             }
 1225:         },
 1226:         output => {
 1227:             type => 'scalar',
 1228:         },
 1229:         _yamltest_hints => {
 1230:             boundary_values => [0, 1],
 1231:             invalid_inputs  => [undef, -1],
 1232:             equivalence_classes => [],
 1233:         },
 1234:         _notes => '...',
 1235:         _analysis => {
 1236:             input_confidence  => 'low',
 1237:             output_confidence => 'unknown',
 1238:             confidence_factors => {
 1239:                 input  => {...},
 1240:                 output => {...},
 1241:             },
 1242:             overall_confidence => 'low',
 1243:         },
 1244:         _fields => {},
 1245:         _modern_features => {},
 1246:         _attributes => {},
 1247:     };
 1248: 
 1249: =head1 METHODS
 1250: 
 1251: =head2 new
 1252: 
 1253: Construct a new SchemaExtractor for a given Perl source file.
 1254: 
 1255:     my $extractor = App::Test::Generator::SchemaExtractor->new(
 1256:         input_file           => 'lib/MyModule.pm',  # Required
 1257:         output_dir           => 'schemas/',         # Optional - only needed if writing schemas
 1258:         verbose              => 1,                  # Default: 0
 1259:         include_private      => 1,                  # Default: 0
 1260:         max_parameters       => 50,                 # Default: 20
 1261:         confidence_threshold => 0.7,               # Default: 0.5
 1262:         strict_pod           => 0|1|2,              # Default: 0 (off)
 1263:         allow_signature_exec => 1,                  # Default: 0 (off)
 1264:     );
 1265: 
 1266: =head3 Arguments
 1267: 
 1268: =over 4
 1269: 
 1270: =item * C<$input_file>
 1271: 
 1272: Path to the Perl source file to analyse. Required. Must exist on disk.
 1273: 
 1274: =item * C<output_dir>
 1275: 
 1276: Directory to write generated schema YAML files. Optional - only
 1277: required if C<_write_schema> will be called. Callers passing
 1278: C<no_write =E<gt> 1> to C<extract_all> do not need to supply it.
 1279: 
 1280: =item * C<verbose>
 1281: 
 1282: Print progress messages to stdout during analysis. Optional, default 0.
 1283: 
 1284: =item * C<include_private>
 1285: 
 1286: Include methods whose names begin with C<_> in the analysis. Optional,
 1287: default 0. Methods whose name begins with C<_new>, C<_init>, or
 1288: C<_build> are always included regardless of this setting (a prefix
 1289: match, so e.g. C<_build_attribute> and C<_init_logger> qualify too,
 1290: matching common Moose builder/initializer naming conventions).
 1291: 
 1292: =item * C<max_parameters>
 1293: 
 1294: Safety limit on the number of parameters analysed per method to prevent
 1295: runaway processing on pathological code. Optional, default 20.
 1296: 
 1297: =item * C<confidence_threshold>
 1298: 
 1299: Minimum confidence score (0.0-1.0) below which a schema is marked with
 1300: C<_low_confidence =E<gt> 1>. Optional, default 0.5.
 1301: 
 1302: =item * C<strict_pod>
 1303: 
 1304: Controls POD/code agreement validation. C<0> disables validation,
 1305: C<1> emits warnings, C<2> croaks on first disagreement. Also accepts
 1306: the strings C<off>, C<warn>, and C<fatal>. Optional, default 0.
 1307: 
 1308: =item * C<allow_signature_exec>
 1309: 
 1310: Opt-in flag allowing extraction of parameter types from a
 1311: L<Type::Params> C<signature_for()> declaration. This requires actually
 1312: running the C<signature_for> expression (sliced from the target
 1313: module's own source) in a forked C<perl -T> process, since
 1314: L<Type::Params> types are runtime objects that cannot be introspected
 1315: statically. Every other extraction path in this module is static
 1316: (L<PPI>-only) analysis that never executes any of the target module's
 1317: code; this is the one exception. Optional, default 0 (the
 1318: C<signature_for> path is silently skipped, with a warning under
 1319: C<verbose>, when off). Only enable this for modules whose code you
 1320: already trust enough to execute.
 1321: 
 1322: =back
 1323: 
 1324: =head3 Returns
 1325: 
 1326: A blessed hashref. Croaks if C<input_file> is missing or does not
 1327: exist on disk.
 1328: 
 1329: =head3 Side effects
 1330: 
 1331: Reads and parses the input file using L<PPI> at construction time.
 1332: 
 1333: =head3 API specification
 1334: 
 1335: =head4 input
 1336: 
 1337:     {
 1338:         input_file           => { type => SCALAR },
 1339:         output_dir           => { type => SCALAR,  optional => 1 },
 1340:         verbose              => { type => SCALAR,  optional => 1 },
 1341:         include_private      => { type => SCALAR,  optional => 1 },
 1342:         max_parameters       => { type => SCALAR,  optional => 1 },
 1343:         confidence_threshold => { type => SCALAR,  optional => 1 },
 1344:         strict_pod           => { type => SCALAR,  optional => 1 },
 1345:         allow_signature_exec => { type => SCALAR,  optional => 1 },
 1346:     }
 1347: 
 1348: =head4 output
 1349: 
 1350:     {
 1351:         type => OBJECT,
 1352:         isa  => 'App::Test::Generator::SchemaExtractor',
 1353:     }
 1354: 
 1355: =cut
 1356:

 1373: 		max_parameters       => $params->{max_parameters}       // $DEFAULT_MAX_PARAMETERS,	# safety limit
 1374: 		strict_pod => _validate_strictness_level($params->{strict_pod}),	# Enable strict POD checking
 1375: 		allow_signature_exec => $params->{allow_signature_exec} // 0,	# opt-in: execute Type::Params signature_for() exprs from the target module
 1376: 	};
 1377: 
 1378: 	# Validate input file exists
 1379: 	unless (-f $self->{input_file}) {
 1380: 		croak(__PACKAGE__, ": Input file '$self->{input_file}' does not exist");
 1381: 	}
 1382: 
 1383: 	return bless $self, $class;
 1384: }
 1385: 
 1386: =head2 extract_all
 1387: 
 1388: Extract schemas for all qualifying methods in the module and return
 1389: them as a hashref.
 1390: 
 1391:     my $schemas = $extractor->extract_all();
 1392: 
 1393:     # Suppress writing .yml files to disk
 1394:     my $schemas = $extractor->extract_all(no_write => 1);
 1395: 
 1396: =head3 Arguments
 1397: 
 1398: =over 4
 1399: 
 1400: =item * C<no_write>
 1401: 
 1402: When true, schema files are not written to C<output_dir>. The returned
 1403: hashref is still fully populated. Useful when the caller wants to
 1404: inspect or augment schemas before deciding whether to write them.
 1405: Optional, default 0.
 1406: 
 1407: =back
 1408: 
 1409: =head3 Returns
 1410: 
 1411: A hashref mapping method name strings to schema hashrefs. Each schema
 1412: contains at minimum the keys C<function>, C<module>, C<input>,
 1413: C<output>, and C<_analysis>. See L</Generated Schema Structure> for
 1414: the full structure.
 1415: 
 1416: =head3 Side effects
 1417: 
 1418: Parses the input file with L<PPI>. Writes one YAML file per method to
 1419: C<output_dir> unless C<no_write> is set. Creates C<output_dir> if it
 1420: does not exist and writing is enabled.
 1421: 
 1422: =head3 Notes
 1423: 
 1424: Private methods (names beginning with C<_>) are excluded unless
 1425: C<include_private =E<gt> 1> was passed to C<new>. Duplicate method
 1426: names are deduplicated with a warning logged to stdout in verbose mode.
 1427: 
 1428: POD/code agreement validation is applied if C<strict_pod> was set in
 1429: C<new>. At level 2 (fatal), the first disagreement causes an immediate
 1430: croak.
 1431: 
 1432: =head3 API specification
 1433: 
 1434: =head4 input
 1435: 
 1436:     {
 1437:         self     => { type => OBJECT, isa => 'App::Test::Generator::SchemaExtractor' },
 1438:         no_write => { type => SCALAR, optional => 1 },
 1439:     }
 1440: 
 1441: =head4 output
 1442: 
 1443:     {
 1444:         type => HASHREF,
 1445:         keys => {
 1446:             '*' => {
 1447:                 type => HASHREF,
 1448:                 keys => {
 1449:                     function  => { type => SCALAR  },
 1450:                     module    => { type => SCALAR  },
 1451:                     input     => { type => HASHREF },
 1452:                     output    => { type => HASHREF },
 1453:                     _analysis => { type => HASHREF },
 1454:                 },
 1455:             },
 1456:         },
 1457:     }
 1458: 
 1459: =cut
 1460:

 6480: #             supported: (1) my ($self, ...) = @_,

 6482: #             (3) my $x = $_[N]. $self and $class

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 7738: # Entry:      $params - hashref keyed by parameter name; modified in place.
 7739: #             $where  - the analysis stage, for the warning text.
 7740: #
 7741: # Exit:       Number of keys dropped.
 7742: #
 7743: # Notes:      A hashref used where a list of key/value pairs was meant
 7744: #             stringifies to "HASH(0x...)" and silently becomes a parameter.
 7745: #             Such a key is never a variable the routine can read, so the
 7746: #             schema would test an argument that does not exist.  Perl hash
 7747: #             keys are always strings, so the string form is what we see.
 7748: #
 7749: # Side effects: Emits a carp warning for each dropped key.
 7750: # --------------------------------------------------
 7751: sub _sanitize_parameter_names {
●7752 → 7755 → 7766 7752: 	my ($self, $params, $where) = @_;
 7753: 
 7754: 	my $dropped = 0;
 7755: 	for my $name (keys %$params) {
 7756: 		my $invalid = ref($name)
 7757: 			|| $name =~ /\A(?:HASH|ARRAY|SCALAR|CODE|GLOB|REF|REGEXP|Regexp|LVALUE|VSTRING|FORMAT|IO)\(0x[0-9a-fA-F]+\)\z/
 7758: 			|| $name =~ /\s/;
 7759: 		next unless $invalid;
 7760: 
 7761: 		carp(__PACKAGE__ . ": ignoring parameter key '$name' from $where: not a parameter name");
 7762: 		delete $params->{$name};
 7763: 		$dropped++;
 7764: 	}
 7765: 
 7766: 	return $dropped;
 7767: }

10070: # _detect_external_object_dependency
10071: #
10072: # Purpose:    Detect whether a method creates or
10073: #             depends on objects from classes other
10074: #             than the current package, by scanning
10075: #             for ->new() calls on named classes
10076: #             and method calls on typed variables.
10077: #
10078: # Entry:      $method_body - method source string.
10079: #                            May be undef.
10080: #
10081: # Exit:       Returns a dependency_info hashref if
10082: #             external object usage is found, or
10083: #             undef otherwise.
10084: #             The hashref may contain:
10085: #             creates_objects (arrayref of class
10086: #             names), uses_objects (arrayref of
10087: #             class names), and package (the primary
10088: #             dependency class).
10089: #
10090: # Side effects: None.
10091: # --------------------------------------------------
10092: sub _detect_external_object_dependency {
●10093 → 10102 → 1010810093: 	my ($self, $method_body) = @_;
10094: 
10095: 	return undef unless $method_body;
10096: 
10097: 	my %dependency_info;
10098: 
10099: 	# Pattern 1: Creates objects of other classes with ->new() or ->create()
10100: 	# Reset pos for global match
10101: 	pos($method_body) = 0;
10102: 	while ($method_body =~ /(\w+(?:::\w+)*)->(?:new|create)\(/g) {
10103: 		my $class = $1;
10104: 		next if $class eq 'main' || $class eq '__PACKAGE__' || $class =~ /^\$/;
10105: 		push @{$dependency_info{creates_objects}}, $class;
10106: 	}
10107: 
●10108 → 10108 → 1011610108: 	if ($dependency_info{creates_objects}) {
10109: 		# Remove duplicates
10110: 		my %seen;
10111: 		$dependency_info{creates_objects} = [grep { !$seen{$_}++ } @{$dependency_info{creates_objects}}];
10112: 		$dependency_info{package} = $dependency_info{creates_objects}[0];
10113: 	}
10114: 
10115: 	# Pattern 2: Calls methods on objects from other classes
●10116 → 10116 → 1014610116: 	if ($method_body =~ /\$(\w+)->\w+\(/) {
10117: 		my %object_vars;
10118: 		# Reset pos for global match â€” the if check above used a
10119: 		# non-/g match so it cannot have advanced pos, but the while
10120: 		# loop's own /g matches still need to start from the beginning.
10121: 		pos($method_body) = 0;
10122: 		while ($method_body =~ /\$(\w+)->\w+\(/g) {
10123: 			$object_vars{$1}++;
10124: 		}
10125: 
10126: 		# Try to determine type of object variables
10127: 		my @object_classes;
10128: 		foreach my $var (keys %object_vars) {
10129: 			# Look for type declarations or assignments
10130: 			if ($method_body =~ /my\s+\$$var\s*=\s*(\w+(?:::\w+)+)->(?:new|create)/) {
10131: 				push @object_classes, $1;
10132: 			} elsif ($method_body =~ /my\s+\$$var\s*=\s*(\w+(?:::\w+)+)->/) {
10133: 				push @object_classes, $1;
10134: 			}
10135: 		}
10136: 
10137: 		if (@object_classes) {
10138: 			$dependency_info{uses_objects} = \@object_classes;
10139: 			$dependency_info{package} = $object_classes[0] unless $dependency_info{package};
10140: 		}
10141: 	}
10142: 
10143: 	# Pattern 3: Receives objects as parameters (type hints in comments/POD)
10144: 	# This would need integration with parameter analysis
10145: 
10146: 	return \%dependency_info if keys %dependency_info;
10147: 	return undef;
10148: }

10243: # --------------------------------------------------
10244: # _extract_default_value
10245: #
10246: # Purpose:    Extract a default value for a named
10247: #             parameter from a method body by
10248: #             matching multiple common Perl default
10249: #             assignment idioms.
10250: #
10251: # Entry:      $param - parameter name string.
10252: #             $code  - method body source string.
10253: #
10254: # Exit:       Returns the cleaned default value
10255: #             scalar on success, or undef if no
10256: #             default assignment pattern is found.
10257: #
10258: # Side effects: None.
10259: #
10260: # Notes:      Eight patterns are tried in order:
10261: #             ||, //=, defined ternary, unless
10262: #             defined, ||=, //, multi-line if
10263: #             !defined, unless defined block.
10264: #             Comment lines are stripped from the
10265: #             code before matching to avoid false
10266: #             positives. Delegates to
10267: #             _clean_default_value for value
10268: #             normalisation.
10269: # --------------------------------------------------
10270: sub _extract_default_value {
●10271 → 10283 → 1029110271: 	my ($self, $param, $code) = @_;
10272: 
10273: 	return undef unless $param && $code;
10274: 
10275: 	# Clean up the code for easier pattern matching
10276: 	# Remove comments to avoid false positives
10277: 	my $clean_code = $code;
10278: 	$clean_code =~ s/#.*$//gm;
10279: 	$clean_code =~ s/^\s+|\s+$//g;
10280: 
10281: 	# Pattern 1: $param = $param || 'default_value'
10282: 	# Also handles: $param = $arg || 'default'
10283: 	if ($clean_code =~ /\$$param\s*=\s*(?:\$$param|\$[a-zA-Z_]\w*)\s*\|\|\s*([^;]+)/) {
10284: 		my $default = $1;
10285: 		$default =~ s/\s*;\s*$//;
10286: 		$default = $self->_clean_default_value($default);
10287: 		return $default if defined $default;
10288: 	}
10289: 
10290: 	# Pattern 2: $param //= 'default_value'
●10291 → 10291 → 1030010291: 	if ($clean_code =~ /\$$param\s*\/\/=\s*([^;]+)/) {
10292: 		my $default = $1;
10293: 		$default =~ s/\s*;\s*$//;
10294: 		$default = $self->_clean_default_value($default);
10295: 		return $default if defined $default;
10296: 	}
10297: 
10298: 	# Pattern 3: $param = defined $param ? $param : 'default'
10299: 	# Also handles: $param = defined $arg ? $arg : 'default'
●10300 → 10300 → 1030810300: 	if ($clean_code =~ /\$$param\s*=\s*defined\s+(?:\$$param|\$[a-zA-Z_]\w*)\s*\?\s*(?:\$$param|\$[a-zA-Z_]\w*)\s*:\s*([^;]+)/) {
10301: 		my $default = $1;
10302: 		$default =~ s/\s*;\s*$//;
10303: 		$default = $self->_clean_default_value($default);
10304: 		return $default if defined $default;
10305: 	}
10306: 
10307: 	# Pattern 4: $param = 'default' unless defined $param;
●10308 → 10308 → 1031510308: 	if ($clean_code =~ /\$$param\s*=\s*([^;]+?)\s+unless\s+defined\s+(?:\$$param|\$[a-zA-Z_]\w*)/) {
10309: 		my $default = $1;
10310: 		$default = $self->_clean_default_value($default);
10311: 		return $default if defined $default;
10312: 	}
10313: 
10314: 	# Pattern 5: $param ||= 'default'

●10323 → 10323 → 1033110323: 	if ($clean_code =~ /\$$param\s*=\s*(?:\$$param|\$[a-zA-Z_]\w*)\s*\/\/\s*([^;]+)/) {
10324: 		my $default = $1;

10327: 		return $default if defined $default;

10350: # _extract_test_hints

10352: # Purpose:    Extract structured test hints from
10353: #             a method's code and schema, including
10354: #             boundary values, invalid inputs, and
10355: #             valid input examples from POD.

10358: #             $schema - schema hashref as built so

10362: #             boundary_values, invalid_inputs,

10369: sub _extract_test_hints {

10374: 		invalid_inputs => [],

10376: 		valid_inputs => [],
10377: 	);
10378:

10387: 		delete $hints{$k} unless @{$hints{$k}};

10391: }

10429: # --------------------------------------------------
10430: # _extract_boundary_value_hints
10431: #
10432: # Purpose:    Extract numeric boundary values from
10433: #             comparison operators in a method body,
10434: #             adding both the boundary value and
10435: #             the value one step either side.
10436: #
10437: # Entry:      $code  - method body source string.
10438: #             $hints - hints hashref (modified in
10439: #                      place via boundary_values key).
10440: #
10441: # Exit:       Returns nothing. Appends to and
10442: #             deduplicates $hints->{boundary_values}.
10443: #
10444: # Side effects: None.
10445: # --------------------------------------------------
10446: sub _extract_boundary_value_hints {
●10447 → 10449 → 1046410447: 	my ($self, $code, $hints) = @_;
10448: 
10449: 	while ($code =~ /\$\w+\s*(<=|<|>=|>)\s*(\d+)/g) {
10450: 		my ($op, $n) = ($1, $2);
10451: 
10452: 		if ($op eq '<') {
10453: 			push @{ $hints->{boundary_values} }, $n, $n+1;
10454: 		} elsif ($op eq '<=') {
10455: 			push @{ $hints->{boundary_values} }, $n, $n+1;
10456: 		} elsif ($op eq '>') {
10457: 			push @{ $hints->{boundary_values} }, $n, $n-1;
10458: 		} elsif ($op eq '>=') {
10459: 			push @{ $hints->{boundary_values} }, $n, $n-1;
10460: 		}
10461: 	}
10462: 
10463: 	# Remove duplicates
10464: 	my %seen;
10465: 	$hints->{boundary_values} = [ grep { !$seen{$_}++ } @{ $hints->{boundary_values} } ];
10466: }

10564: # --------------------------------------------------
10565: # _clean_default_value
10566: #
10567: # Purpose:    Normalise a raw default value string
10568: #             extracted from code or POD into a
10569: #             clean Perl scalar, handling quoted
10570: #             strings, numeric literals, boolean
10571: #             keywords, empty containers, and
10572: #             undef.
10573: #
10574: # Entry:      $value     - raw value string.
10575: #                          May be undef.
10576: #             $from_code - true if the value was
10577: #                          extracted from source
10578: #                          code (affects escape
10579: #                          sequence handling).
10580: #
10581: # Exit:       Returns the cleaned value:
10582: #               undef   for undef or unparseable
10583: #               {}      for empty hashrefs
10584: #               []      for empty arrayrefs
10585: #               integer for whole numbers
10586: #               float   for decimal numbers
10587: #               1 or 0  for boolean keywords
10588: #               string  for everything else
10589: #
10590: # Side effects: None.
10591: # --------------------------------------------------
10592: sub _clean_default_value {
●10593 → 10605 → 1061210593: 	my ($self, $value, $from_code) = @_;
10594: 
10595: 	return unless defined $value;
10596: 
10597: 	# Remove leading/trailing whitespace
10598: 	$value =~ s/^\s+|\s+$//g;
10599: 
10600: 	# Remove parenthetical notes like "(no password)" only if there's content before them
10601: 	$value =~ s/(\S+)\s*\([^)]+\)\s*$/$1/;
10602: 	$value =~ s/^\s+|\s+$//g;
10603: 
10604: 	# Handle chained || or // operators - extract the rightmost value
10605: 	if ($value =~ /\|\||\/{2}/) {
10606: 		my @parts = split(/\s*(?:\|\||\/{2})\s*/, $value);
10607: 		$value = $parts[-1];
10608: 		$value =~ s/^\s+|\s+$//g;
10609: 	}
10610: 
10611: 	# Remove trailing semicolon if present
●10612 → 10615 → 1062410612: 	$value =~ s/;\s*$//;
10613: 
10614: 	# Handle q{}, qq{}, qw{} quotes
10615: 	if ($value =~ /^qq?\{(.*?)\}$/s) {
10616: 		$value = $1;
10617: 	} elsif ($value =~ /^qw\{(.*?)\}$/s) {
10618: 		$value = $1;
10619: 	} elsif ($value =~ /^q[qwx]?\s*([^a-zA-Z0-9\{\[])(.*?)\1$/s) {
10620: 		$value = $2;
10621: 	}
10622: 
10623: 	# Handle quoted strings
●10624 → 10624 → 1064710624: 	if ($value =~ /^(['"])(.*)\1$/s) {
10625: 		$value = $2;
10626: 
10627: 		if ($from_code) {
10628: 			# In regex captures from source code, escape sequences are doubled
10629: 			# \\n in capture needs to become \n for the test
10630: 			$value =~ s/\\\\/\\/g;
10631: 		}
10632: 
10633: 		# Only unescape the quote characters themselves
10634: 		$value =~ s/\\"/"/g;
10635: 		$value =~ s/\\'/'/g;
10636: 
10637: 		# If NOT from code (i.e., from POD), interpret escape sequences
10638: 		unless ($from_code) {
10639: 			$value =~ s/\\n/\n/g;
10640: 			$value =~ s/\\r/\r/g;
10641: 			$value =~ s/\\t/\t/g;
10642: 			$value =~ s/\\\\/\\/g;
10643: 		}
10644: 	}
10645: 
10646: 	# Sometimes trailing ) is left on
●10647 → 10647 → 1065210647: 	if($value !~ /^\(/) {
10648: 		$value =~ s/\)$//;
10649: 	}
10650: 
10651: 	# Handle Perl empty hash (must be before numeric/boolean checks)
●10652 → 10652 → 1065710652: 	if ($value =~ /^\{\s*\}$/) {
10653: 		return {};
10654: 	}
10655: 
10656: 	# Handle Perl empty list/array
●10657 → 10657 → 1066210657: 	if ($value =~ /^\[\s*\]$/) {
10658: 		return [];
10659: 	}
10660: 
10661: 	# Handle numeric values
●10662 → 10662 → 1067110662: 	if ($value =~ /^-?\d+(?:\.\d+)?$/) {
10663: 		if ($value =~ /\./) {
10664: 			return $value + 0;
10665: 		} else {
10666: 			return int($value);
10667: 		}
10668: 	}
10669: 
10670: 	# Handle boolean keywords
●10671 → 10671 → 1067610671: 	if ($value =~ /^(true|false)$/i) {
10672: 		return lc($1) eq 'true' ? 1 : 0;
10673: 	}
10674: 
10675: 	# Handle Perl boolean constants
●10676 → 10676 → 1068310676: 	if ($value eq '1') {
10677: 		return 1;

10679: 		return 0;

●10683 → 10683 → 1068810683: 	if ($value eq 'undef') {

10698: 		# return $value;
10699: 	}

10702: }

10704: # --------------------------------------------------
10705: # _validate_pod_code_agreement
10706: #
10707: # Purpose:    Compare POD parameter documentation
10708: #             against code-inferred parameters and
10709: #             return a list of disagreements when
10710: #             strict_pod mode is enabled.
10711: #
10712: # Entry:      $pod_params  - hashref of parameters
10713: #                            from POD analysis.
10714: #             $code_params - hashref of parameters
10715: #                            from code analysis.
10716: #             $method_name - method name string,
10717: #                            used for context in
10718: #                            error messages.
10719: #
10720: # Exit:       Returns a list of disagreement
10721: #             strings. Returns an empty list if
10722: #             all parameters agree.
10723: #
10724: # Side effects: None.
10725: #
10726: # Notes:      Type mismatches are classified as
10727: #             either 'compatible' (e.g. integer vs
10728: #             number) or 'incompatible' via
10729: #             _types_are_compatible. $self and
10730: #             $class are excluded from undocumented
10731: #             parameter warnings in appropriate
10732: #             context.
10733: # --------------------------------------------------
10734: sub _validate_pod_code_agreement {
●10735 → 10742 → 1081310735: 	my ($self, $pod_params, $code_params, $method_name) = @_;
10736: 
10737: 	my @errors;
10738: 
10739: 	# Get all parameter names from both sources
10740: 	my %all_params = map { $_ => 1 } (keys %$pod_params, keys %$code_params);
10741: 
10742: 	foreach my $param (sort keys %all_params) {
10743: 		my $pod = $pod_params->{$param} || {};
10744: 		my $code = $code_params->{$param} || {};
10745: 
10746: 		# A =head3|4 Input formal spec is authoritative for the parameter's
10747: 		# existence, type, optionality and pattern: heuristic code analysis may
10748: 		# legitimately disagree.  Its bounds are still checked below, because a
10749: 		# formal minimum and a code maximum that do not overlap leave the merged
10750: 		# schema with no valid value.
10751: 		unless(($pod->{_source} // '') eq SOURCE_INPUT_SPEC) {
10752: 			# Check if parameter exists in both
10753: 			if (exists $pod_params->{$param} && !exists $code_params->{$param}) {
10754: 				push @errors, "Parameter '\$$param' documented in POD but not found in code signature";
10755: 				next;
10756: 			}
10757: 
10758: 			if(!exists $pod_params->{$param} && exists $code_params->{$param}) {
10759: 				if($param =~ /^(class|pkg|proto|klass)$/i) {
10760: 					# class invocant, not a user-facing parameter
10761: 					next;
10762: 				}
10763: 				if($param eq 'self') {
10764: 					# instance invocant, not a user-facing parameter
10765: 					next;
10766: 				}
10767: 				push @errors, "Parameter '\$$param' found in code but not documented in POD";
10768: 				next;
10769: 			}
10770: 
10771: 			# Compare types if both exist
10772: 			if ($pod->{type} && $code->{type} && $pod->{type} ne $code->{type}) {
10773: 				if (!$self->_types_are_compatible($pod->{type}, $code->{type})) {
10774: 					push @errors, "Type mismatch for '\$$param': POD says '$pod->{type}', code suggests '$code->{type}' (incompatible)";
10775: 				} else {
10776: 					push @errors, "Type difference for '\$$param': POD says '$pod->{type}', code suggests '$code->{type}' (compatible)";
10777: 				}
10778: 			}
10779: 
10780: 			# Compare optional status if both exist
10781: 			if (exists $pod->{optional} && exists $code->{optional} &&
10782: 				$pod->{optional} != $code->{optional}) {
10783: 				my $pod_status = $pod->{optional} ? 'optional' : 'required';
10784: 				my $code_status = $code->{optional} ? 'optional' : 'required';
10785: 				push @errors, "Optional status mismatch for '\$$param': POD says '$pod_status', code suggests '$code_status'";
10786: 			}
10787: 
10788: 			# Check regex patterns
10789: 			if ($pod->{matches} && $code->{matches} && $pod->{matches} ne $code->{matches}) {
10790: 				push @errors, "Pattern mismatch for '\$$param': POD says '$pod->{matches}', code suggests '$code->{matches}'";
10791: 			}
10792: 		}
10793: 
10794: 		# Check constraints (min/max), including a formal spec's bounds.
10795: 		if (defined $pod->{min} && defined $code->{min} && $pod->{min} != $code->{min}) {
10796: 			push @errors, "Min constraint mismatch for '\$$param': POD says '$pod->{min}', code suggests '$code->{min}'";
10797: 		}
10798: 
10799: 		if (defined $pod->{max} && defined $code->{max} && $pod->{max} != $code->{max}) {
10800: 			push @errors, "Max constraint mismatch for '\$$param': POD says '$pod->{max}', code suggests '$code->{max}'";
10801: 		}
10802: 
10803: 		if (defined $pod->{min} && defined $code->{max} && $pod->{min} > $code->{max}) {
10804: 			push @errors, "Constraint contradiction for '\$$param': POD says min '$pod->{min}', code suggests max '$code->{max}' (no valid value)";
10805: 		}
10806: 
10807: 		if (defined $code->{min} && defined $pod->{max} && $code->{min} > $pod->{max}) {
10808: 			push @errors, "Constraint contradiction for '\$$param': code suggests min '$code->{min}', POD says max '$pod->{max}' (no valid value)";
10809: 		}
10810: 	}
10811: 
10812: 	# Return errors (empty array if no errors)
10813: 	return @errors;
10814: }

10816: # --------------------------------------------------
10817: # _validate_strictness_level
10818: #
10819: # Purpose:    Validate and normalise the strict_pod
10820: #             option value accepted by new() into
10821: #             an integer level: 0 (off), 1 (warn),
10822: #             or 2 (fatal).
10823: #
10824: # Entry:      $val - the raw value passed to
10825: #                    strict_pod in new(). May be
10826: #                    undef, a number, or a string.
10827: #
10828: # Exit:       Returns 0, 1, or 2.
10829: #             Croaks if the value is not recognised.
10830: #
10831: # Side effects: None.
10832: # --------------------------------------------------
10833: sub _validate_strictness_level {
10834: 	my $val = $_[0];
10835: 
10836: 	return 0 unless defined $val;
10837: 
10838: 	# Numeric
10839: 	return 0 if $val =~ /^(0|off|none)$/i;
10840: 	return 1 if $val =~ /^(1|warn|warning)$/i;
10841: 	return 2 if $val =~ /^(2|fatal|die|error)$/i;
10842: 
10843: 	croak("Invalid value for --strict-pod: '$val' (use off|warn|fatal)");
10844: }
10845: 
10846: # --------------------------------------------------
10847: # _types_are_compatible
10848: #
10849: # Purpose:    Determine whether two type strings
10850: #             are compatible for POD/code agreement
10851: #             checking, allowing semantically
10852: #             equivalent types (e.g. 'integer' and
10853: #             'number') to coexist without
10854: #             triggering a strict POD warning.
10855: #
10856: # Entry:      $pod_type  - type string from POD.
10857: #             $code_type - type string from code.
10858: #
10859: # Exit:       Returns 1 if compatible, 0 otherwise.
10860: #
10861: # Side effects: None.
10862: # --------------------------------------------------
10863: sub _types_are_compatible {
●10864 → 10880 → 1088510864: 	my ($self, $pod_type, $code_type) = @_;
10865: 
10866: 	# Exact match is always compatible
10867: 	return 1 if $pod_type eq $code_type;
10868: 
10869: 	# Define compatibility matrix
10870: 	my %compatible_types = (
10871: 		'integer' => ['number', 'scalar'],
10872: 		'number' => ['scalar'],
10873: 		'string' => ['scalar'],
10874: 		'scalar' => ['string', 'integer', 'number'],
10875: 		'arrayref' => ['array'],
10876: 		'hashref' => ['hash'],
10877: 	);
10878: 
10879: 	# Check if code_type is compatible with pod_type
10880: 	if (my $allowed = $compatible_types{$pod_type}) {
10881: 		return grep { $_ eq $code_type } @$allowed;
10882: 	}
10883: 
10884: 	# Check if pod_type is compatible with code_type
●10885 → 10885 → 1088910885: 	if (my $allowed = $compatible_types{$code_type}) {
10886: 		return grep { $_ eq $pod_type } @$allowed;
10887: 	}
10888: 
10889: 	return 0;	# Not compatible
10890: }
10891: 
10892: =head2 generate_pod_validation_report
10893: 
10894: Generate a human-readable report of all POD/code disagreements found
10895: across a set of extracted schemas.
10896: 
10897:     my $schemas = $extractor->extract_all(no_write => 1);
10898:     my $report  = $extractor->generate_pod_validation_report($schemas);
10899:     print $report;
10900: 
10901: =head3 Arguments
10902: 
10903: =over 4
10904: 
10905: =item * C<$schemas>
10906: 
10907: A hashref of method name to schema hashref as returned by
10908: C<extract_all>. Required.
10909: 
10910: =back
10911: 
10912: =head3 Returns
10913: 
10914: A string containing the full validation report, or a single line
10915: confirming all methods passed if no disagreements were found.
10916: 
10917: =head3 Side effects
10918: 
10919: None.
10920: 
10921: =head3 Notes
10922: 
10923: Only methods whose schemas contain a C<_pod_validation_errors> key
10924: (populated when C<strict_pod> is 1 or 2) appear in the report. If
10925: C<strict_pod> was 0 when C<extract_all> was called, this method will
10926: always return the all-passed message.
10927: 
10928: =head3 API specification
10929: 
10930: =head4 input
10931: 
10932:     {
10933:         self    => { type => OBJECT,  isa => 'App::Test::Generator::SchemaExtractor' },
10934:         schemas => { type => HASHREF },
10935:     }
10936: 
10937: =head4 output
10938: 
10939:     { type => SCALAR }
10940: 
10941: =cut
10942: 
10943: sub generate_pod_validation_report {
●10944 → 10947 → 1095910944: 	my ($self, $schemas) = @_;
10945: 
10946: 	my @reports;
10947: 	foreach my $method_name (sort keys %$schemas) {
10948: 		my $schema = $schemas->{$method_name};
10949: 
10950: 		if (my $errors = $schema->{_pod_validation_errors}) {
10951: 			push @reports, "Method: $method_name";
10952: 			push @reports, "  Severity: " . ($schema->{_pod_disagreement} ? 'warning' : 'fatal');
10953: 			push @reports, "  Errors:";
10954: 			push @reports, map { "    - $_" } @$errors;
10955: 			push @reports, '';
10956: 		}
10957: 	}
10958: 
●10959 → 10959 → 010959: 	if (@reports) {
10960: 		return join("\n", "POD/Code Validation Report:", '=' x 40, '', @reports);
10961: 	} else {
10962: 		return 'POD/Code Validation: All methods passed consistency checks.';
10963: 	}
10964: }
10965: 
10966: =head2 _log
10967: 
10968: Log a message if verbose mode is on.
10969: 
10970: =cut
10971: 
10972: sub _log {
10973: 	my($self, $msg) = @_;
10974: 
10975: 	print "$msg\n" if $self->{verbose};
10976: }
10977: 
10978: =head1 NOTES
10979: 
10980: C<SchemaExtractor> uses heuristic analysis of Perl source and POD to infer
10981: parameter types and constraints. Inference accuracy improves with
10982: well-documented modules; C<=head3 Input> / C<=head4 Input> formal specs are
10983: parsed at highest priority and override all heuristics.
10984: 
10985: A lone name in a C<=head4 input> block is treated as the first positional
10986: argument when the body reads a positional argument (C<< $_[0] >>, a list
10987: assignment from C<@_>, or a C<shift>) and does not read an argument hash
10988: (C<my %args = @_>, a hashref dereference, or C<< $_[0]{...} >>); the
10989: documented name is then a label for that argument, as C<name> is for
10990: C<$_[0]> in L<Lingua::EN::NameCase/nc>.  Two or more names, and a body that
10991: only forwards C<@_>, remain named arguments.
10992: 
10993: The output is always a best-effort schema suitable as a starting template;
10994: review and augment the generated YAML before using it as a definitive
10995: specification. Pass it to L<App::Test::Generator> to generate fuzz harnesses.
10996: 
10997: =head1 TODO
10998: 
10999: Extend C<=head4 Input> parsing to cover the C<enum>/C<memberof> constraint
11000: synonym (union types, e.g. C<scalar | scalarref>, are already handled by
11001: C<_map_formal_input_type>).
11002: 
11003: =head1 SEE ALSO
11004: 
11005: =over 4
11006: 
11007: =item * L<App::Test::Generator> - Generate fuzz and corpus-driven test harnesses
11008: 
11009: Output from this module serves as input to that module.
11010: So with well-documented code, you can automatically create your tests.
11011: 
11012: =item * L<App::Test::Generator::Template> - Template of the file of tests created by C<App::Test::Generator>
11013: 
11014: =back
